          examiner circled, i.e., which letters the patient identified correctly.). Coordinating center
          personnel will double check the data entered by the examiner and data entry staff.

        5.2.3.B     Visual acuity training and certification

          The goal of the certification process is to standardize methodology for refraction and
          visual acuity measurement. All visual function examinations must be performed by
          study-certified technicians. The principal investigator at each site is responsible for
          ensuring that the appropriate personnel are identified, trained and certified. A qualified
          visual function examiner (SL) from the Clinical Coordinating Center (Proctor Foundation)
          will certify the rooms and technicians at each site.

          Technicians are expected to perform the refraction and visual acuity tests on at least
          one and possibly more non-study subjects according to protocol requirements. The
          examiner will determine whether or not the candidate executes the study protocol
          accurately for each procedure. A checklist (Visual Acuity/Refraction Certification Form,
          Section B.1) containing required procedures will be used to facilitate this process.

          Room certification will be performed and recorded (Room Certification Form, section
          B.2) to ensure that all study rooms meet illumination, equipment, and distance
          requirements. Logs should be kept to document dates of light box bulb replacement.

          Certification is valid for a period 18 months (± 2 months) from the date of
          certification. The process should begin as soon as possible, as technicians must be
          certified before the first study subject is seen. A minimum of two certified technicians
          are required at each site. The visual acuity measurement schedule may be found on
          the Study Forms Completion Schedule.

5.3. Ophthalmic procedures

     5.3.1. Grading Cataracts
     The study ophthalmologist will grade cataracts at every clinical exam based on the Age-
     Related Eye Disease Study (AREDS) clinical lens grading system (Chew et al., 2010).
     Pupils must be dilated maximally and lenses should be examined at the slit lamp for the
     presence and severity of three types of opacity: nuclear, cortical and posterior
     subcapsular. For each type of opacity, the examiner should compare the lens being
     examined with the AREDS 2008 Clinical lens opacity standard photographs, of increasing
     severity. In each series, “1” indicates clinical presence, “2” indicates clinical significance,
     and “3” indicates severe occurrence. Half grades may be given, for example if a nuclear
     opacity was thought to be half way between the first and second standard photographs, a
     decimalized grade of “1.5” could be assigned.
                                                                                                            61

First-line Antimetabolites as Steroid-sparing Treatment (FAST) Uveitis Trial        January 6, 2017 - Version 4.5
Manual of Operation and Procedures



    The study ophthalmologists will refer to laminated photos of the scale in real time when
    making the clinical assessment. If no cataract is present, he/she will indicate this on the
    clinical exam form.

    The following categories of severity are present for all types of opacity (nuclear, cortical
    and posterior subcapsular): <1, 1, 1.5, 2, 2.5, >3.




    *Clinical grading adapted from: Chew EY, KIM J, Sperduto RD et al. Evaluation of the Age-Related Eye Disease
    Study Clinical Lens Grading System AREDS Report No. 31. Ophthalmology 2010: 11; 2112-2119.

    5.3.2. Grading inflammation
    Slit lamp examination and dilated funduscopic exam are important parts of our study:
    assessments of anterior chamber cells and vitreous haze will determine the patient’s course
    of treatment. All of our study physicians are fellowship-trained uveitis specialists who have
    participated in prior uveitis studies.

    The study ophthalmologist at each site will be required to perform an eye examination at
    each study visit. This examination should be similar to that performed in the routine care of
    uveitis patients. Several components will be assessed in detail per study protocol. The
    grading schemes to be used for these assessments are adapted from the Standardization of
    Uveitis Nomenclature (SUN) Working Group.2

    Anterior Chamber Cells
    SUN Working Group Grading Scheme for Anterior Chamber Cells
                          Grade                                            Cells in Field*
                              0                                                   <1
                            0.5+                                                  1-5
                             1+                                                  6-15
                             2+                                                 16-25
                             3+                                                 26-50
                             4+                                                  >50
    *Field size is a 1mm by 1mm slit lamp beam. Grading should be done with the highest magnification and
    illumination and conducted in a completely dark room, prior to dilation.

                                                                                                                62

```text
First-line Antimetabolites as Steroid-sparing Treatment (FAST) Uveitis Trial           January 6, 2017 - Version 4.5
Manual of Operation and Procedures



    Anterior Chamber Flare
    Anterior Chamber Flare will be assessed by slit lamp exam but will not be part of our study
    criteria for controlled inflammation.

    SUN Working Group Grading Scheme for Anterior Chamber Flare
                   Grade                                   Description
                     0                                        None
                    1+                                        Faint
                    2+                        Moderate (iris and lens details clear)
                    3+                         Marked (iris and lens details hazy)
                    4+                          Intense (fibrin or plastic aqueous)
    *Adapted from: Hogan MJ, Kimura SJ, Thygeson P. Signs and symptoms of uveitis: I. Anterior
    Uveitis. AM J Ophthal 1964;47:155-170; Grading should be conducted in a completely dark room

    Vitreous Cells
    There is currently no accepted standard for grading vitreous cells, which will not be part of
    our study criteria for controlled inflammation. However, we will collect this information using
    the grading scheme below, which is adapted from the MUST trial:

                        Grade                                              Cells in Field*
                           0                                                 No cells
                         0.5+                                               0 to 5 cells
                          1+                                                6-10 cells
                          2+                                                11-20 cells
                          3+                                                21-50 cells
                          4+                                                    >50
 *Field size is a 1mm by 0.5mm beam; Grading should be conducted in a completely dark room; These cells are
 graded by slit lamp exam after dilation.

    Vitreous Haze
    Vitreous haze will be assessed using an indirect ophthalmoscope set at mid-power and a
    20D lens. Fundus examination will be done with the last view being the area around the
    optic disc. The examiner should then compare this view to the photograph (Figure 1) and
    determine the level of vitreous haze. The National Eye Institute System for grading
    vitreous haze will be used for all primary and secondary outcome measures. Additionally,
    vitreous haze will be assessed separately on the 9-point grading scale recently published
    by Davis et al., known as the Miami scale. The examiner should compare the vitreous
    haze view with the Miami photograph. An exploratory analysis will correlate
    measurements from the NEI and Miami scales. Clinical measurements of vitreous haze
    will also be compared to objective assessments done by grading of fundus photos by the
    reading center.

    If the physician thinks that the grading of vitreous haze is affected by a significant media
    opacity (i.e. a dense/mature cataract) they should indicate on the Clinical Exam Form that
    they cannot make a reliable judgment of vitreous haze due to the opacity.




                                                                                                                   63


```

                              0                                                    Clear

                             1+                              Opacities without obscuration of retinal details

## Extracted relationships and matching main-paper keys

### PC-01: ocular-inflammation measurement, definition and observer

[DOC-003 p129](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=129), MOP65: Figure 2 Miami nine-point vitreous haze has panels labeled 0,1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8. The grade images show increasing haze, without numerical patient data. Retinal/choroidal active inflammation is assessed clinically with ancillary imaging if needed; active vasculitis posterior to equator qualifies, requiring fluorescein confirmation under SUN. Macular edema alone does not qualify; bullous serous retinal detachment with any accompanying vitreous/retinal/choroidal inflammation qualifies. Text also specifies ≥1+ anterior chamber inflammation, vitreous haze and/or other retinal/choroidal lesions aside from macular edema as active.

Inter-observer agreement is assessed at baseline (Phases I/II), month6 (Phases I/II), month12 (Phase I), or treatment failure, whichever earlier. Two certified physicians measure anterior chamber cells/flare, vitreous cells/haze and retinal/choroidal lesions. Inter-observer variation will be reported; primary study ophthalmologist measurements are used for primary analysis. [p130](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=130) requires ≥80% open-book exam score for physicians/coordinators. Physicians grade five patients before trial; differences >1 grade trigger more grading until five consecutive patients are within one level for each measurement. Patients with zero inflammation in all areas by both graders do not count. Repeated annually, with duplicate measurements at month6 and/or12. Matching main keys: primary success inflammation components; inflammation grading scale and masking; eye-level measurement versus participant-level success.

### PC-02: OCT endpoint acquisition and units

[p130](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=130): spectral-domain OCT monitors macular thickness and thickness change; Heidelberg Spectralis preferred with same scanner throughout a patient's study; Zeiss Cirrus permitted if Heidelberg unavailable. Heidelberg each-eye scans: high-speed volume centered on macula 20°×20°,49 sections,16 ART frames; 7-line scan30°×5°,7 sections,25 ART frames. [p131](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=131) repeats parameters and requires quality score at least20; incomplete/poor scans repeated. Central subfield thickness is recorded separately for each eye, printed and used promptly to assess macular edema. No numerical thickness units or cutoffs appear on these pages.

[p133](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=133), visually read: one Macular Cube scan and one5 Line Raster centered on macula required for each eye; selected preset is printed “Scan Macular Cube 512x218 HD”. The last numeral is218 as directly visible; no normalization to an instrument specification was performed. Dummy DOB1/1/2000 and example patient ID1Q001 are file identifiers, not participant observations. [p134](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=134): repeat procedure for “5 Line Raster HD”; image maintained in upper half of screen; signal strength at least5; inadequate scans repeated. Obtain Macular Thickness analysis and5 Line Raster analysis; record central subfield thickness on data sheet promptly for real-time physician macular-edema assessment. Raw SD-OCT saved locally and on separate removable drive; export both eyes and transmittal form; at least2 operators per site trained on Heidelberg/Zeiss. [pp132,134](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=132) export examples encode study/patient/visit/date and have no analysis value. Matching main keys: central macular thickness/change; baseline edema and OCT measurement scale/instrument.

### PC-03: photographic measurements

[p135](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=135): color fundus photography at baseline,6 months,12 months or treatment failure, including fundus reflex and non-stereoscopic images. Vitreous haze graded by masked graders using NEI and Miami scales; photographers certified by UPRC. [p143](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=143) repeats both-eye photography schedule; [p144](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=144) reading center evaluates edema type, epiretinal membrane and vitreous haze on NEI/Miami fundus photographs. Matching main keys: masked inflammation grading, macular edema, vitreous haze secondary measurements.

### PC-04: laboratory collection and treatment failure

[p135](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=135): hematology/chemistry before enrollment and throughout study. Hemoglobin and/or hematocrit, platelets,WBC,%neutrophils,%lymphocytes; CD4 lymphocytes at UCSF/AEH/KKESH only, baseline/month3/6/12. AST(SGOT),ALT(SGPT),creatinine,pregnancy test. Baseline measurements within4 weeks before enrollment; follow-up within visit window, every study visit. Non-serious abnormalities (example AST/ALT twice ULN) immediately stop drugs; allow one month to regain eligibility. If normal within one month restart drug per protocol, possibly sooner if labs normalize; otherwise declare treatment failure, treat at physician judgment and still follow sixth-month visit.

[p136](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=136), visually read: lab abnormalities meeting SAE threshold immediately stop drugs, declare treatment failure, continue follow-up for6 months, and do not switch to Phase II. Non-serious AE includes any unfavorable occurrence after receipt of drug regardless of causal relation. Examples: increased IOP >24 mmHg; AST/ALT ≥2 but <5 times ULN; creatinine ≥1.5 to <2 mg/dL; WBC below2.5 (unit not printed here); concurrent accident/illness, increased pre-existing symptoms, GI upset/nausea/vomiting/fatigue. Serious adverse events include ≥5 times ULN AST/ALT, death, non-elective surgery/hospitalization, myocardial infarction/stroke, life-threatening drug experience/event, persistent/significant disability/incapacity, cancer,seizure, congenital anomaly, permanent damage and intervention preventing impairment/damage. Threshold inequality signs come from visual inspection. All AEs recorded each visit with onset/resolution log and patient symptoms reviewed while physician remains masked. Investigator SAE narrative submitted to Medical Monitor/PI/manager within24 hours.

[p137](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=137): for nonlaboratory SAEs, failure cannot be declared until Medical Monitor judges drug relatedness; monitor decides whether true drug-related failure and whether safe to switch Phase II. If unsafe, physician judgment treatment and six-month follow-up. Investigator may stop medication and obtain failure-visit measurements before attribution, but waits to complete formal treatment assessment/declaration until monitor instruction. Statistician informs DSMC of SAEs by arm every6 months. Patient death reported within24h; deceased participant's arm available to DSMC if appropriate. Matching main keys: safety/tolerability component of primary success/failure; liver-function adverse events; failure reasons; crossover eligibility; six-month endpoint ascertainment.

### PC-05: data structure, missingness and compliance

[p138](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=138): coordinator cross-checks forms within24h; scan/email within10 days; two independent operators at Aravind double-enter REDCap forms, merge within10 days of receipt. Five automated checks address ID checksum, out-of-range fields, illogical entries, incompleteness and extreme numeric values. This is data processing, not a missing-outcome analysis rule.

[p139](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=139): Excel analysis exports have single variable-name header; columns are variables and rows observations. Missing values explicitly string NA; prefer category names over integer codes (examples male1/female2). Codebook lists form, question, possible values, summary statistics and units (example logMAR) with dated version control. No supplied workbook or cached/formula values occur. Data Analysis Committee merges unmasked data with randomization list at designated times; uses R analysis/report-generation tools. Entry delays >30 days trigger operational investigation/retraining/reassignment; monthly recruitment/compliance/retention/data-quality reports. These clauses give no imputation model or numeric estimate.

[p141](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=141): record missed doses in individual calendars and medication log, bring calendar/medication to each visit; pill counts each visit. Charts checked twice a year. Prefer same primary ophthalmologist at all visits. [p142](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=142): database locked after cleaning before conclusion analyses; randomization list distributed to pharmacist and unmasked senior physicians for emergencies. Refractionists record both-eye best spectacle-corrected VA as letters read and Snellen equivalent each visit; OCT both eyes all visits. Matching main keys: analysis population/data preparation, missing outcomes, adherence, VA units, randomization and masking. Randomization-holder clause is mapped together with disclosed v4.5 amendment below; no discrepancy conclusion is drawn.

### PC-06: interim/statistical decision design

[p144](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=144): DAC designs SAP and performs unmasked analyses; prespecified interim analyses when one-third and two-thirds of primary outcome data collected; provides data for DSMC stopping-guideline evaluation. DSMC is independent/NEI-appointed, unmasked, approves protocol before enrollment, receives arm-labeled reports at both one-third/two-thirds interim points and SAE reports every6 months. No alpha boundary or sample size is stated on this page. Matching main keys: interim testing/accrual fractions, stopping/multiplicity rules, masking of analysis personnel.

### PC-07: protocol revision chronology and analysis-specification amendment

[p148](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=148): v2.0 September26,2012 in response to first DSMC feedback changes primary regression to predictors treatment arm and site only. v2.1 March1,2013 changes abnormal hemoglobin eligibility threshold ≤10 to ≤9 (unit unstated here), Week2 window ±3days and Week2 labs/AE checklist schedule. v2.2 May1,2013 adds surgery planned within6 months as exclusion, PhaseI months6–12 laboratory assessments at months7,8,10,11, modifies non-serious/serious lab-event wording. v2.3 July22,2013 adds Melbourne/Mexico City, delays enrollment/data collection3 months, adds NCT number. v2.4 August19,2013 switches drug procurement to UCSF pharmacy and adds Northwestern. Administrative site/timeline amendments give context, without an analysis-result comparator. Matching main keys: primary adjusted model/covariates, eligibility, lab-related failure and protocol version.

### PC-08: versions2.4–3.1 and population eligibility

[p149](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=149): v2.4 removes identical unlabeled bottles; names medications but uses opaque bags to mask observers; specifies Proctor double entry and excludes another ocular condition anticipated to dictate treatment. v2.5 October1,2013 permits NSAIDs alongside study-dose methotrexate through clarified high-dose warning. v2.6 December2,2013 excludes periocular/intravitreal steroid injection within4 weeks. v3.0 March24,2014 adds hepatitisB panel, switches TumblingE to ETDRS letter charts; recent failed steroid taper window14days→4weeks, abnormal AST/ALT/CBC window14days→4weeks, TB/treponemal/hepatitis tests60days→90days. v3.1 May8,2014 broadens criteria.

[p150](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=150), v3.1 old criterion: active inflammation within90days, at least one eye, ≥2+AC cells or ≥2+vitreous haze or active retinal/choroidal lesions. Old qualifying circumstances: active after4weeks high-dose1mg/kg prednisone-equivalent or4+weeks post injection; failed taper with ≥1grade increase or inactive→active lesions within4weeks; known qualifying uveitis condition. New active lookback180days, same ≥2+ thresholds; one of five circumstances: (1) active after4weeks1mg/kg oral prednisone-equivalent; (2) same inflammation increase during taper in preceding180days; (3) oral prednisone-equivalent printed in layout as “³10mg/day” for at least preceding90days; (4) long-acting injection4weeks–180days earlier; (5) Behcet posterior involvement,multifocal choroiditis/panuveitis,serpiginous choroidopathy,birdshot retinochoroidopathy,diffuse retinal vasculitis,VKH bullous serous retinal/choroidal detachments, or sympathetic ophthalmia (continues p151). The direct rendered source also shows a superscript3-like glyph before10mg/day; retain its literal rendering rather than silently converting it to an inequality. The eligibility statement is mapped in full; the glyph is a source typography observation, not an unread source unit. Matching main keys: trial eligible inflammation/prednisone use/population, visual-acuity chart, baseline disease and steroid exposure.

### PC-09: versions3.2–4.3 and intervention/outcome measurements

[p151](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=151): v3.2 July29,2014 adds KKESH. v3.3 December12,2014 clarifies PhaseI prednisone taper goal maintain7.5mg/day, not lower (subject to clinician judgment); acute VKH active ≥4weeks qualifies; enrollment eye exam/VA may be up to7days before baseline. v3.4 January13,2015 removes HIV from mandatory specimen list within60days (active infection remains excluded; testing at investigator discretion). v3.5 April6,2015 clarifies daily/BID terms, NEI-VFQ every site plus IND-VFQ India, Miami name for Davis, persistent macular-edema injection allowed in first90days of BOTH PhasesI/II, NEI vitreous haze scale for enrollment; stale lab-window text updated14days→4weeks and60days→90days. v4.0 April30,2015 begins here and continues next page.

[p152](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=152): v4.0 updates data-entry location to Madurai, removes month6 from PhaseI months6–12 window table. v4.1 June26,2015 permits IMT other than corticosteroids when more than12months before enrollment without previous adverse events; excludes use in last12months, prior methotrexate/MMF intolerance or safety issues, prior failure to control ocular/other inflammation using either drug, any prior biologic. Forms target7days→10days. v4.2 September21,2015 adds Pondicherry. v4.3 October2,2015 restores Zeiss Cirrus to OCT procedures, and p153 clarifies TumblingE wording. Matching main keys: steroid-sparing primary success threshold, prior IMT exclusion, adjunct injections, QoL outcomes, visual-acuity assessments and OCT device.

### PC-10: versions4.4–4.5 safety/randomization/crossover amendments

[p153](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=153): v4.4 February26,2016 adds §2.5.2 clarification: if laboratory results remain at or above non-serious AE threshold for28days, classify as serious AE and immediately declare treatment failure. CD4 required only AEH,KKESH,UCSF baseline/month3/6/12. v4.5 January6,2017 removes randomization list holders from §2.4, adds secure online REDCap randomization performed by coordinators; emergency contacts access assignments in database. Adds §4.1.2 coordinating-center permission for exceptional PhaseII entry outside two-week window. Removes primary/secondary clinical eye-exam forms from PhaseII baseline if entry on day different from PhaseI failure visit; adds online-randomization paragraph §4.3.2. Matching main keys: primary failure definition, lab AE thresholds, randomization method, PhaseII crossover timing/measurements. These are disclosed amendments and must accompany any version comparison.

# SAP formula visual transcription supplement

Scope: DOC-004, PLAN, joi190092supp3_prod.pdf, exact PDF pages 22, 23, 24, 26, 34, 63 and 64. This bounded additional mapping supplements extraction/parts/sap_001_064.md; it does not replace that map, diagnose findings, or adjudicate candidates. Seven assigned pages were freshly rendered and visually inspected in full. No additional page is required to close the assigned formula-transcription qualification.

## Engine and derivative provenance

Direct Poppler pdftoppm 26.01.0, CPU, was used after consulting tooling_record.md. For each N in 22,23,24,26,34,63,64 the command was:

`pdftoppm -f N -l N -singlefile -r 140 -png joi190092supp3_prod.pdf .ai_paper_validation/review_1_6_1/preprocessing/sap_formula/page-N`

The outputs are preprocessing/sap_formula/page-22.png, page-23.png, page-24.png, page-26.png, page-34.png, page-63.png and page-64.png below the review directory. Each was opened with view_image. An additional directly rendered p.34 formula detail was opened with view_image:

`pdftoppm -f 34 -l 34 -singlefile -r 300 -x 1000 -y 1500 -W 700 -H 250 -png joi190092supp3_prod.pdf .ai_paper_validation/review_1_6_1/preprocessing/sap_formula/page-34-formula-detail`

No OCR or fallback environment was needed. No supplied source or reused derivative was modified. Formula expressions below transcribe printed symbols rather than substituting assumed quantile conventions.

## PDF page 22 — printed page 21, 29 January 2013

[Source](../../../joi190092supp3_prod.pdf#page=22). No displayed sample-size formula appears on this page. Section 3.4.1 states target sample size 216, approximately 80% power, difference of 20% in the proportion achieving control of inflammation at six months between methotrexate and mycophenolate mofetil. The model-validation material on this page is already mapped in the original extraction.

## PDF page 23 — printed page 22, 29 January 2013

[Source](../../../joi190092supp3_prod.pdf#page=23). Equation (5) is printed:

`2N = 4(Z_{alpha} + Z_{beta})^2 pbar(1-pbar) / (p_c-p_i)^2`.

Here alpha and beta are Greek subscripts on capital Z. The alpha subscript is simply alpha, with no printed /2 or 1-minus component; beta is simply beta. The numerator p symbols carry bars. The adjacent text explicitly defines alpha as significance level 0.05, two sided; beta as one minus power, desired power 80%; p_c as methotrexate success probability 0.4; p_i as mycophenolate mofetil success probability 0.6; and `pbar = (p_i+p_c)/2`. Text assumes 10% loss to follow-up in the first six months and states approximately 108 per group, `2 x 108 = 216`. The primary objective is superiority of mycophenolate to methotrexate; clinically meaningful effect is 20%.

The table is visually confirmed in full:

| Success Drug A | 80% power detectable effect | 80% power success Drug B | 90% power detectable effect | 90% power success Drug B |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 20% | 18% | 38% | 21% | 41% |
| 30% | 20% | 50% | 23% | 53% |
| 40% | 20% | 60% | 23% | 63% |
| 50% | 20% | 70% | 22% | 72% |
| 60% | 19% | 79% | 21% | 81% |

The 40% row is bold. The page also states simulation confirms adequate logistic-regression sample sizes, and final critical value will be adjusted slightly because of interim analysis. Sample-size reassessment is considered subject to DSMB approval.

## PDF page 24 — printed page 23, 29 January 2013

[Source](../../../joi190092supp3_prod.pdf#page=24). Survival sample-size expression is printed:

`N = (Z_{alpha}+Z_{beta})^2 [phi(lambda_C)+phi(lambda_I)] / (lambda_I-lambda_C)^2`.

Both lambda subscripts are capital C and capital I. As in Equation (5), the Z subscripts are Greek alpha and beta with no /2 or 1-minus term. The auxiliary expression is printed:

`phi(u) = u^2 / (1-e^{-uT})`.

Lambda_C is defined as methotrexate hazard; lambda_I as mycophenolate hazard; T is censoring time, 6 months. Adjacent text describes approximately 3.5-month median success time for mycophenolate, then explicitly states in its parenthetical note `lambda_C is log(2)/3.5 mo. for this calculation`. Both statements are retained as printed. With 10% loss, 108 in each group is stated to provide 80% power to detect difference of 2.47 months in the expected difference. Alpha 0.05, two sided, is explicit.

Other numeric context: anatomic subgroup difference 25% with power in excess of 80% by Equation (5); additional 5% loss from 6 to 12 months beyond initial 10%, approximately 78% power for 20% difference at 12 months; vision change SD 6.5 letters, sample size 108, approximately 80% power for 2.63-letter difference using R power.t.test. ## Confirmed structured relationships

### Design and population

Pages 9–10 specify patient randomization to oral MTX versus oral MMF, observer-masked comparative effectiveness design, site-block randomization, 216 total and 108 per arm. Phase I lasts zero to six months; six-month successes continue the same drug to twelve months. Initial failures switch agents and have six additional months of follow-up or terminate at second failure. Site recruitment targets on p.11 are Madurai 100, Coimbatore 71, San Francisco 15, Oregon 30. Recruitment November 2012–May 2015 is described as 2.5 years; six-month follow-up through December 2015 includes a one-month visit window, maximum follow-up is listed through July 2015 with a one-month window, and lock/analyze/disseminate through April 2016. These calendars are recorded as administrative facts, not converted into analysis inputs.

Site block sizes on p.13 are 4, 6 or 8 with equal probability, known only to the biostatistician. Pages 14–15 confirm patients/coordinators know assigned drug; clinical eye examiners, visual-acuity examiners, OCT operators, fundus photographers and graders remain masked. A masking break triggers a deviation form; the patient continues in the trial. Patient is the allocation unit; success applies to both eyes, while ocular measurement forms are eye-specific.

### Primary endpoint and complete secondary inventory

Pages 10 and 15: primary Aim 1 is the rate of overall corticosteroid-sparing treatment success six months after enrollment. Page 15 requires controlled ocular inflammation in BOTH eyes, oral prednisone ≤7.5 mg/day, topical 1% prednisolone acetate ≤2 drops/day, and no tolerability or safety issue warranting discontinuation. Table 2 requires anterior-chamber cells ≤0.5+ (SUN), vitreous haze ≤0.5+ (NEI) and no active choroidal/retinal lesions. Lesions are independent of macular edema. The image confirms inclusive ≤ symbols; OCR '<' renderings are transcription aids only.

The p.10 secondary outcomes are: Aim 2 success six months after switching following failure within the first six months, using the same endpoint; time to inflammation control; time to treatment success; five-month success sustained at least 28 days to six months; twelve-month Phase-I success; Phase-I success during months 6–12 with complete corticosteroid discontinuation; six-month Phase-II success; Phase-II success without corticosteroids; change in best spectacle-corrected visual acuity; presence of macular edema; change in macular thickness; clinical Davis vitreous-haze change; photographic NEI/Davis vitreous-haze change; efficacy in intermediate/posterior/panuveitis subgroups; proportion discontinuing for intolerability; adverse-event rate; proportion discontinuing for serious adverse events; health/vision quality of life; occurrence of dose reduction; efficacy among VKH patients. No imposed key-secondary designation is added.

### Eligibility and anatomic classification

Pages 11–12 define intermediate disease by vitreous-predominant inflammation, posterior by retina/choroid and panuveitis by anterior chamber plus vitreous plus retina/choroid without a predominant site. Macular edema does not change anatomic classification. Anterior plus intermediate is distinct from panuveitis. Historical noninfectious intermediate, anterior/intermediate, posterior or panuveitis in at least one eye is required. Within 90 days, ≥2+ anterior-chamber cells, ≥2+ vitreous haze or active retinal/choroidal lesions in at least one eye is required. At enrollment ≥1+ cells/haze or active lesions (including bullous serous detachment) in at least one eye is required. Additional requirement: persistent activity after four weeks at 1 mg/kg prednisone equivalent, taper-associated reactivation in the preceding fourteen days, or a chronic condition requiring steroid-sparing therapy. Initial willingness is min(1 mg/kg,60 mg/day).

Page 13 exclusions: infectious cause; posterior segment not examinable in both eyes due to opacity/synechiae; bilateral IOP <5 mm Hg for >3 months; prior nonprednisone immunosuppression; periocular/intravitreal steroid in past two months; fluocinolone implant in either eye within three years; intraocular surgery within thirty days; hand-motion acuity or worse in the better eye; age <16; pregnancy/breastfeeding/planning conception; unwillingness to abstain from alcohol (two drinks/month permitted); cancer; systemic autoimmune disease anticipated to dictate treatment. CBC within fourteen days excludes WBC ≤2,500, platelets ≤75,000 or hemoglobin ≤10; AST/ALT ≥2×ULN or creatinine ≥1.5 also excludes. Pregnancy testing required except postmenopause. Infection exclusion/testing windows are sixty days for tuberculosis, HIV, syphilis, hepatitis B/C; named tests include tuberculin or IGRA, chest film, RPR/VDRL, FTA-ABS, HBsAg and HCV antibody. Latent, not active, tuberculosis remains eligible.

### Failure, dropout, missing visits and rescue

Pages 15–17: discontinuation for efficacy, tolerability or safety may produce failure at any time. Efficacy includes failure to reach ≤7.5 mg/day prednisone and ≤2 topical drops/day by six months or clinician-defined futility of persistent uncontrolled inflammation. Intolerability can trigger dose reductions and ultimately failure. SAE requires immediate exit without rescue. Nonserious laboratory abnormalities trigger an immediate medication hold, permit one month to regain eligibility, and permit resumption if normal by then; otherwise failure and exit. Other nonserious events may lead to temporary interruption. If interruption leaves inflammation uncontrolled or prednisone >7.5 mg/day at six months, efficacy failure remains possible.

Dropout is explicit unwillingness to participate or death, not a missed visit or temporary drug discontinuation. Follow-up is attempted after missed visits and temporary interruptions. Drug-related death counts as failure; unrelated death counts as dropout, under medical-monitor judgment. These statements define event handling; no missing-outcome imputation model or statistical estimator is specified on pp.1–32.

The p.18 image confirms: monthly efficacy/tolerability/safety/taper assessment; SAE→failure; futility→alternative antimetabolite Phase II; intolerability→reduction I→if persistent reduction II→if persistent alternative Phase II, otherwise continue per protocol. Six-month controlled inflammation plus ≤7.5 mg prednisone and ≤2 topical drops/day→success and original treatment months 6–12; reassess the same control/steroid limits at twelve months. Six-month non-success→alternate Phase II→six months later apply the same limits→success/failure. Footnotes identify the same inflammation criteria and topical 1% prednisolone acetate or equivalent.

### Randomized intervention and adherence

Page 19 Tables 4/5, visually confirmed, and p.20 narrative: first two weeks MTX 15 mg once weekly (day 1, split morning/evening on the same day), then MTX 25 mg weekly; MMF 500 mg BID on every day 1–7, then 1.5 g BID every day, preferably empty stomach. MTX recipients receive folic acid 1 mg on every day. Reduction I: MTX 20 mg/week or MMF 1 g BID; reduction II: MTX 15 mg/week or MMF 500 mg BID; folic acid remains 1 mg/day. Reduction only for intolerability or safety; otherwise maintain highest dose through follow-up. Inability to escalate after the introductory two weeks declares intolerability failure; in Phase I permits switching, in Phase II requires exit. Persistent intolerability after the two reductions can lead to switch/exit; early lack of efficacy or AE can terminate treatment.

Page 21: introductory supply fourteen days, maintenance thirty days; both supplies issued at baseline and thirty-day resupply at monthly visits through five months. Switching repeats the same dispensing schedule. Six-month first-line successes receive 120 days of medication. Diaries and pill counts measure adherence. A missed MTX weekly dose may be taken next day; if not, wait until next week. A missed MMF dose returns to usual schedule. These schedules are recorded independently of effect estimates.

### Corticosteroid and adjunctive schedules

Pages 22–23: prednisone initially min(1 mg/kg,60 mg/day) for 2–4 weeks; gradual taper to ≤7.5 mg/day and hold at 7.5 through six months. Equivalent alternative corticosteroid is permitted only for a prednisone contraindication. Full antimetabolite effect is expected after 6–8 weeks, so flares during the first three months do not necessarily constitute failure. Clinician futility can nevertheless declare earlier failure. Page 22 uses the phrase Phase II for further reduction if continuing the same medication; p.23 titles the optional extension schedule Phase I (6–12 months). Both printed phrases are preserved without introducing a new relationship.

Table 6 p.23, dose mg/day : duration weeks : decrement mg/day: 60:2–4:10; 50:1:10; 40:1:5; 35:1:5; 30:1:5; 25:1:5; 20:1:2.5; 17.5:1:2.5; 15:1:2.5; 12.5:1:2.5; 10:1:2.5; 7.5:hold. Footnotes start at the weight/cap rule and hold 7.5 until six months; no improvement after four weeks permits best-judgment supplemental treatment.

Table 7 optional extension: 7.5 mg/day for at least one week then decrement 2.5; dose <7.5 and ≥5 for 2–4 weeks decrement 1–2.5; <5 and ≥2.5 for 2–4 weeks decrement 1.5–2.5; <2.5 and ≥1 for 2–4 weeks decrement 1; zero final row. Mild reactivation rises up to 1+ anterior cells/haze, severe to 2+ or higher; lesion severity at clinician discretion. Severe rescue increases prednisone up to 1 mg/kg; mild rescue up to twice the flare dose until control then re-taper. Stable but nonimproving inflammation after four weeks is nonresponsive; hold current prednisone an additional week, then possible efficacy failure. Switching restarts the initial taper; second-agent failure exits.

Table 8 p.24 topical frequency/day : duration weeks: 8:1–2; 6:1; 4:1; 3:1; 2:1; 1:1; none. Goal is complete discontinuation, ≤2/day prednisolone acetate 1% or equivalent acceptable. Supplements: calcium 500 mg three times/day, vitamin D 400–800 IU/day. IOP-lowering/cycloplegic adjuncts are recorded; steroid injections, IV therapy, added immunosuppression or surgery require a deviation form if needed.

### Laboratory threshold table

Page 16 Table 3 remains as printed, including inclusive/exclusive bounds and duration:

| Measure | Nonserious | Serious |
|---|---|---|
| Leukocytes | >1,000 and <2,500/µL lasting <1 month | <2,500 lasting >1 month or ≤999/µL |
| Platelets | 20,000–75,000/µL lasting <1 month | ≤20,000/µL or <75,000/µL lasting >1 month |
| Hemoglobin | >6.4 to <10 g/dL | ≤6.4 g/dL |
| AST/ALT | 2–5 times reference ULN | >5 times reference ULN |
| Creatinine | ≥1.5 to <2 mg/dL | ≥2 mg/dL |

### Visit windows, forms and measurement timing

Pages 25–27: enrollment sequence is consent then unique ID then concealed allocation lookup then labeled dispensing. Coordinator reviews medication diaries/AE/QOL before physician; acuity, anterior eye and IOP precede dilation; posterior examination/OCT/photos/laboratories follow. Baseline-I is treatment day 1, visits originate from randomization, and minimum spacing is two weeks. Phase-I/II initial schedule: baseline, week 2, months 1–6; months 1–5 have ±15 days, month 6 −15/+30 days. Monthly dates use the average number of days/month. Table 10 extension uses month 6 ±15, month 9 ±15, month 12 −15/+30. Phase-II baseline is first alternate-drug day, may coincide with failure, and resets visit origin. Non-study Phase-I failure requires baseline-II immediately or within seven days; Phase-II failure exits. All these exact stated windows are retained.

Page 28 full forms matrix columns are Phase-I baseline, months 1–5/9, failure, months 6/12; Phase-II baseline, months 1–5, failure, month 6. Consent/eligibility only Phase-I baseline; baseline history at both baselines; clinical eye, visual-acuity and OCT at every listed column; photos and QOL at both baselines, both failure columns, Phase-I months 6/12 and Phase-II month 6; laboratory at both baselines, both regular follow-up columns, Phase-I months 6/12 and Phase-II month 6, with no failure-column X. Treatment assessment at all columns except Phase-I baseline. AE and dropout forms are conditional across follow-up, deviation forms conditional throughout. Failure may be at scheduled or non-study visits.

Pages 29–32: baseline laboratories may be obtained within fourteen days before/on randomization; other baseline data at baseline-I. CBC differential records neutrophil/lymphocyte percentages; serum AST/ALT/creatinine and blood/urine pregnancy testing except postmenopause. Follow-up data within visit windows. Right/left visual-acuity forms are distinct. Baseline-II uses failure-visit data when appropriate, updates history, and is restricted to efficacy/tolerability failures, with SAE excluded. Non-study visits require no specimens; eye/assessment forms if failure and AE forms if event. Extended first-line study data are recorded months 9/12 or failure although clinical visits may be more frequent.

QOL instruments: SF-36 for all, NEI-VFQ-25 in US and IND-VFQ in India. Collection at baseline visits, six months or failure whichever first, and twelve months for maintained Phase-I success; Phase-II collection at six months or failure. Self-administration preferred; coordinator can read questions verbatim for poor vision/illiteracy and record responses. No QOL score formula or result is specified within pp.1–32.

## Structured quantitative and statistical confirmation

### Measurement procedures and scale definitions

[PDF pp.33–40](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=33): contact-lens delay 30 minutes, manifest refraction ordinarily at 4 m, below 20/200 defined by missing at least two largest-line letters moves to 1 m. Acuity bands are 6/3–6/24 (20/10–20/80), 6/30–6/60 (20/100–20/200), and below 6/60 (below 20/200). Lens-guide settings in diopters: higher band sphere +0.50/−0.37/+0.50, replacement increments +0.50/−0.25/+0.50; cylinder axis cross-cylinder 0.50, power cross-cylinder 0.25, increments ±0.25; spherical refinement ±0.37 with increments ±0.25. Middle band sphere ±1.00, increments ±1.00; axis and power cross-cylinder 1.00, increments ±1.00; refinement ±0.50, increments ±0.50. Lower band sphere ±2.00, increments ±2.00; axis/power cross-cylinder 1.00 and increments ±1.00; refinement ±1.00, increments ±1.00. At 1 m add +0.75 D to beginning approximate refraction. Initial cylinder axes 90°,180°,45°,135°; cross-cylinder comparisons ±45° from existing axis; first neutral comparison may shift axis 15°. Axis-step table repeated on pp.35,37,39: cylinder <1 D→10°; 1–<2→5°; 2–<3→3°; 3–<5→2°; 5–<8→1°. Beginning cylinder power at least 1 D, each 0.5 D cylinder change receives opposite-sign 0.25 D sphere compensation to preserve spherical equivalent. Record right and left eyes. These are measurement-standardization values, not observed trial estimates.

[PDF pp.40–44](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=40): three charts (right eye Chart 1, left eye Chart 2, refraction Chart R), each 14 tumbling-E lines; geometric size means arithmetic logMAR progression. Third row 0.8 logMAR, 45 letters, 20/125. Testing is separate per eye after refraction at each study visit. Stop on a row with ≤3 letters correct from row 3 downward. Fewer than 10 letters at 4 m triggers 1 m testing; test first six rows at 1 m; fewer than 10 there triggers low-vision testing. Count fingers: 1,2,5 fingers at 1 m in random order, at least 3/5 presentations. Hand motion: 0.5 m, about one back-and-forth/second, at least 4/5 presentations. Light perception: four maximal-intensity presentations at 0.5 m, examiner judgment. Printed scoring starts p.43 with “10 or more letters of the first line” at 4 m, continuing p.44 with score = correct 4 m letters +30. Another printed p.44 condition is ≤3 correct letters on largest line at 4 m→score = correct 1 m letters; ≤3 at 1 m→low-vision conversion to logMAR according to referenced Visual Acuity Calculation Table. Retain these printed conditions separately from the testing transitions. Highest attainable 4 m score 100; Snellen equivalent uses hardest line with at least 4/5 letters. Actual low-vision conversion values are not present on these assigned pages.

Operational calibration on p.41: box 62.9×65.4×17.8 cm; third-row height 124.5±5.1 cm; ambient illumination ≤15 foot-candles with box off; two 20 W tubes, described 5% decline in first 100 hours and another 5% in next 2000, pre-use approximately 96 hours, annual replacement. These are equipment instructions rather than result-bearing analyses. Certification p.44 lasts 18±2 months; at least two technicians/site.

[PDF p.45](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=45): anterior-chamber cells, 1×1 mm field: grade 0 <1, 0.5+ 1–5, 1+ 6–15, 2+ 16–25, 3+ 26–50, 4+ >50. Flare grades 0 none, 1+ faint, 2+ moderate/clear details, 3+ marked/hazy details, 4+ intense/fibrin or plastic aqueous. Flare is not controlled-inflammation criterion. Vitreous cells, 1×0.5 mm field: 0 no cells, 0.5+ 0–5, 1+ 6–10, 2+ 11–20, 3+ 21–50, 4+ >50; vitreous cells also not controlled-inflammation criterion. Grading in dark room; vitreous-cell grading after dilation.

[PDF pp.46–47](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=46): NEI haze grades 0 clear; 0.5+ slight disc-margin blur and loss of nerve-fiber striations/reflex; 1+ opacities without retinal-detail obscuration; 2+ mild nerve/vessel blur; 3+ significantly blurred but visible; 4+ dense opacity obscuring nerve head. NEI is used for all primary/secondary outcomes. Davis nine-point scale additionally measured, exploratory correlation between NEI/Davis planned, clinical haze compared with objective fundus-photo grading. Figure 2 p.47 is nine images labeled 0,1,2 / 3,4,5 / 6,7,8; no patient counts, estimates, intervals, P values or score-conversion table. At month 6/12 or earlier failure, two certified physicians measure AC cells/flare and vitreous cells/haze; interobserver variation reported, primary study ophthalmologist used for primary analysis. No agreement statistic is specified here.

[PDF pp.47–51](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=47): each site chooses Heidelberg Spectralis or Zeiss Cirrus and retains it for all patients throughout study; both eyes measured. Heidelberg volume 20°×20°,49 sections,16 ART frames; seven-line preset 30°×5°,7 sections,25 ART frames; scan quality ≥20; central subfield thickness recorded, baseline reference used. Zeiss macular cube preset printed 512×218 plus five-line raster; signal strength ≥5; central subfield thickness recorded. Immediate interpretation determines presence/absence of macular edema, with no numerical diagnostic thickness cutoff specified on these pages. Fundus photos at baseline,6/12 months or failure; masked graders apply NEI/Davis scales. No observed thickness or treatment effect appears here.

### Safety, outcome classification and exclusions

[PDF pp.52–53](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=52): hematology includes hemoglobin/hematocrit, platelets, WBC and neutrophil/lymphocyte percentages; chemistry AST/ALT, creatinine and pregnancy test. Baseline labs within 14 days of visit; follow-up within visit window. Nonserious laboratory abnormality (example twice AST/ALT ULN) causes immediate drug stoppage, permits one month to regain eligibility, and permits earlier restart when labs normalize. Otherwise failure and study exit; uncontrolled inflammation during that interruption at month 6 can cause efficacy failure. SAE causes immediate stoppage and study exit. Nonserious examples span p.52–53: IOP >24 mm Hg; AST/ALT >2 but <5×ULN; creatinine ≥1.5 to <2 mg/dL; WBC below 2.5 as printed (no unit in this example). SAE list: AST/ALT ≥5×ULN, death, nonelective surgery/hospitalization, myocardial infarction/stroke, life-threatening drug experience, persistent/significant disability/incapacity. SAE and death notification within 24 hours. These support primary-success safety/tolerability classification and safety-result matching keys. No new baseline exclusion definition is introduced in pp.33–64; do not infer one from adverse-event examples.

### Analysis data, inferential scope and interim rules

[PDF pp.55–56](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=55): analysis sets are rectangular Excel exports, each row observation, each column variable, header matches REDCap. Missing values explicitly NA; categorical strings preferred; codebook defines units including logMAR. This is data coding, not an imputation, missing-at-random assumption or analysis-population rule. DAC merges unmasked data with allocation list and uses R; no primary model, covariate set, test sidedness, confidence level, effect measure, sensitivity model, subgroup hypothesis, sample-size formula or power inputs are specified in these assigned pages. Monthly recruitment/compliance/retention/data-quality reports are operational monitoring, not interim efficacy analyses. Missed-dose diary and pill counts at each visit define compliance data; no adherence-based exclusion threshold is stated.

[PDF pp.59–61](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=60): both-eye VA/refraction/OCT each visit and fundus timing are restated. DAC performs unmasked analyses and prespecified interim when one third of patients complete study; provides data for DSMC evaluation of stopping guidelines. DSMC authorized to implement conservative early stopping. No numerical stopping boundary, alpha-spending schedule, interim test statistic or multiplicity adjustment is supplied in these pages. Final MOP version identity on p.64 is not an analysis amendment specification by itself.

Matching main-paper keys for these confirmed relationships: both-eye primary inflammation grading; selected observer for primary analysis; six-month success safety/tolerability exclusion; visual-acuity/logMAR change; central macular thickness and edema; photographic/clinical haze; adverse events; adherence; planned interim analysis. No main-paper values were reread or compared in this assigned confirmation.

No candidate diagnosis was performed.

Coverage: 32/32 pages opened and confirmed; 31 reusable-text units plus one fresh-required embedded-image unit (p.47) whose retained current direct-source render was visually confirmed; mapped 32. Scientific gaps: 0. Derivative gaps: none outstanding in the assigned scope. Scope-specific absent definitions (low-vision conversion values, agreement statistic, inferential model and numerical stopping boundary) remain explicitly absent here and must be assessed from the rest of the supplied package by their assigned mappers. No Office workbook, formula/cached-value pair or CSV exists in these assigned units. No full native appendix is duplicated.

## Structured matching facts

The following are confirmations of existing mapping, not new statistical results. Matching keys refer to main-paper design, primary treatment-success endpoint and failure subtypes, randomized doses/reductions, anatomic subgroup efficacy, secondary vision/mac edema/QoL/safety outcomes, and analysis-population follow-up. No power formula, confidence level, hypothesis test, multiplicity rule or fitted model is stated on these32 pages.

- Design/population: [pp72–73](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=72) patient-level216/108-per-arm,site block allocation,observer masking; [pp75–79](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=75) eligibility and SUN anatomic categories. These define intended randomized population and anatomy strata rather than reported results.
- Endpoint: [p80](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=80) bilateral controlled inflammation at6 months,cells≤0.5+,NEI haze≤0.5+,no active posterior lesions/retinal vasculitis beyond equator,7.5mg prednisone,≤2 topical drops,no injections after90 days,no safety/tolerability/futility failure. [p83](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=83) separately prints≤7.5mg in12-month and PhaseII boxes. Exact location-specific dose text is retained without imposing a harmonized definition.
- Secondary hierarchy/list: [p73](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=73) 6-month switch success,same primary/secondary criteria as Aim1;time to control and success;5-month success sustained≥28 days to6 months;PhaseI12-month success;6–12-month complete steroid discontinuation;BSCVA,mac edema presence,mac thickness change;clinical Miami haze and photograph NEI/Miami haze change;intermediate/posterior/panuveitis and retinal-vasculitis efficacy;intolerance discontinuation,AE rate,SAE discontinuation,health/vision QoL. [p74](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=74) adds dose reduction,VKH efficacy,two-step and zero-inflammation definitions and injection proportions. No inferential model is specified here.
- Intervention: [pp84–86](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=84) MTX15→25mg/week,optional20→15mg/week reductions;MMF1→3g/day,optional2→1g/day reductions;introductory2 weeks. BID for MTX applies to one day/week;MMF daily. [pp88–91](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=88) prednisone and topical schedules,medically necessary flexibility and single permitted macular-edema injection per first90-day phase.
- Failure and observation: [pp80–82](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=80) discontinuation/missing visit does not by itself establish dropout/failure;related death counted failure,unrelated death dropout;early failure follow-up retained. [pp94–95](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=94) allowable visit windows and PhaseII clock reset after failure.

### Safety thresholds: Table3, [PDF p81](../../../joi190092supp2_prod.pdf#page=81)

| Parameter | Nonserious threshold | Serious threshold |
|---|---|---|
| Leukocytes | >1000 to<2500/µL | ≤1000/µL |
| Platelets | 20000 to75000/µL | <20000/µL |
| Hemoglobin | ≥6.5 to<9g/dL | <6.5g/dL |
| AST or ALT | 2 to<5 times reference ULN | ≥5 times reference ULN |
| Creatinine | ≥1.5 to<2mg/dL | ≥2mg/dL |

Any nonserious laboratory abnormality unresolved28 days becomes serious and failure due to safety. The displayed platelet interval is transcribed as printed,without adding unprinted inequality marks. Laboratory persistence/safe-switching and nonlaboratory Medical Monitor relatedness decisions remain distinct.

# DOC-003 PDF pages 97–128: bounded confirmation

## Scope, evidence and result

COMPLETE: all 32 assigned PDF pages of `joi190092supp2_prod.pdf`, corresponding to Manual of Operation and Procedures printed pages 33–64, January 6, 2017, version 4.5. Content role is PLAN throughout. No source outside this disjoint assignment was scientifically inspected. The current mapper artifact `protocol_065_128.md` was used only for its structured scope/provenance/relationships and assigned-page locator rows. No earlier checker, candidate, screening or report was read.

The source-matched reusable asset `.ai_paper_validation/review_1_5_1/preprocessing/protocol_pp097_128_layout.txt` contains 92,278 characters. Six retained current-run OCR files total 17,040 characters, giving 109,318 characters for the assigned text/OCR scope. All six retained source-rendered PNGs were visually opened: PDF pages 105,107,109,116,118,120. Each contains quantitative refraction instructions and a five-row cylinder-axis table; none is blank. Partition: 26 reusable-backed pages + 6 fresh-required pages = 32; mapped 32. No unresolved scientific coverage gap.

Retained derivatives were generated directly using Poppler pdftotext/pdftoppm 26.01.0 and Tesseract 5.5.0 CPU English `--psm 6`, as recorded in `protocol_065_128.md` and `tooling_record.md`. For each of the six pages, the earlier current-run commands were `pdftotext -layout -f N -l N joi190092supp2_prod.pdf .../protocol_b/page-N-layout.txt`, `pdftoppm -r 120 -f N -l N -singlefile -png joi190092supp2_prod.pdf .../protocol_b/page-N`, and `tesseract .../protocol_b/page-N.png .../protocol_b/page-N-ocr -l eng --psm 6`. This confirmation performed no repeated extraction, rendering or OCR. No fallback environment, Python extraction, web or GPU was used. All source, reusable and other current-run artifacts remain unchanged.

## Structured quantitative and design facts

### Eligibility, follow-up and measurement timing

Pages97–98 define the baseline observation window and randomization prerequisite; ≥1+ anterior chamber cells or ≥1+ NEI vitreous haze or active retinal/choroidal inflammation within14days may establish active inflammation even when subsequently improved by steroids. Routine CBC/AST/ALT/creatinine/pregnancy laboratory results use4weeks; hepatitis B/C, TB skin/IGRA/chest radiograph and syphilis use90days. These are observation/eligibility rules, not efficacy estimates. Pages99–101 require retained follow-up regardless of medication compliance and study-window compliance; labs monthly, CD4 selected sites at baseline/3/6/12months; continued Phase-I records at9/12months or failure. Phase-II transition≤2weeks; drug-related SAE requires Medical Monitor decision about safety of switching. Failure patients not entering PhaseII remain followed6months. Main-paper matching keys: baseline inclusion, randomized population, treatment failure, follow-up and crossover.

Page102 QoL defines SF-36 and NEI-VFQ-25 for all, IND-VFQ for Indian patients; baseline,6months or failure whichever first,12months for continued success, and both early failure and6month collection if failure occurs before6months. Self administration preferred, otherwise questions read verbatim. Main matching key: quality-of-life secondary outcome and time point. Non-study failures retain minimum eye/treatment/QoL/medication/AE data; no mandatory specimen.

### Refraction, chart identity and acuity scoring

Letter procedure headings label5.2.A as PRIMARY FOR LETTER VISION CHARTS, while the text defines visual acuity as a secondary trial outcome. Tumbling-E is5.2.B. Both measure separately by eye after refraction, with prior clinical/treatment records unavailable to examiner. Both use4m and change to1m for refraction if≥2 letters missed on top20/200(6/60) line; add+0.75 D sphere when refracting at1m. Contact-lens removal wait30minutes. Main matching keys: best-corrected visual acuity/logMAR secondary outcome and measurement definition; these headings describe chart procedures, not endpoint hierarchy.

The three-band trial-lens guide occurs on103 and115:

| Acuity and distance | Sphere tests/replacements (D) | Cylinder axis/power tests and power changes (D) | Sphere refinement tests/changes (D) |
|---|---|---|---|
|20/10–20/80 (6/3–6/24),4m|+0.50→+0.50;−0.37→−0.25;repeat+0.50|axis0.50;power0.25;changes±0.25|tests±0.37;changes±0.25;repeatplus|
|20/100–20/200 (6/30–6/60),4m|tests/changes±1.00;repeatplus|axis/power1.00;changes±1.00|tests/changes±0.50;repeatplus|
|<20/200 (<6/60),1m|tests/changes±2.00;repeatplus|axis/power1.00;changes±1.00|tests/changes±1.00;repeatplus|

Axis testing uses90°/180°,then45°/135°; cross-cylinder preference positions±45° from cylinder axis; initial tie→15° rotation. If negative-cylinder power would be introduced, remove it and retest positive cylinder90° away. The identical source table on105,107,109,116,118,120 is visually confirmed:

| Cylinder power (D) | Axis step |
|---|---|
|<1.00|10°|
|1.00 to<2.00|5°|
|2.00 to<3.00|3°|
|3.00 to<5.00|2°|
|5.00 to<8.00|1°|

With cylinder≥1 D, each0.5 D cylinder change is accompanied by0.25 D sphere of opposite sign to preserve spherical equivalent. Trial lenses may change if acuity improves. Refinement finishes with a plus-lens check. Page117 retains its literal+0.50 D fallback retest amid±1 D medium-acuity sphere instructions; no new scientific relationship is inferred from that procedural wording.

Page103 calls refraction ChartR version2110. Page110 lists Charts1,2,R as2110,2111,2112 respectively,14high-contrast lines, geometric progression of size/arithmetic logMAR progression; Chart1 right eye,Chart2 left eye,R subjective refraction. Both printed chart-number statements remain in the prior map and are confirmed without imposing an interpretation. Tumbling-E versions are2305,2305a,2305b respectively,14lines and same eye assignment; refractionR2305b on114 agrees with121. Charts are measurement instruments; no reported outcome value is printed here.

Letter VA on112: <20letters at4m→retest first6rows at1m; zero letters1m→low vision. Page113 assigns score calculation to biostatistician without an explicit numeric letter-score formula in this assigned letter subsection. Tumbling-E on123: stop any row from Row3 down if≤3 identified; <10letters4m→retest first6rows1m; <10letters1m→low vision. Pages124–125 independently print literal score rules: ≥10letters **of the first line** read at4m→4mcount+30; ≤3letters of largest line4m→1mcount; ≤3letters1m→low vision; maximum4m score100. These wording/threshold distinctions were already retained in the current mapping and are confirmed, with no substituted formula. Low-vision values are converted to logMAR by the cited Visual Acuity Calculation Table; that table is not within this assignment, so its numeric conversions are not inferred. Snellen equivalent uses hardest line with≥4of5 responses. Entry records row counts at4m/1m and low-vision category, not identities of circled letters; double checking specified.

Low vision in both subsections: count fingers at1m,random1/2/5 fingers,five presentations,≥3of5; hand motion0.5m,about1back/forth per second,five presentations,≥4of5; light perception0.5m,maximal indirect-ophthalmoscope intensity,four presentations, examiner judgment. Vision technicians certified18months±2,minimum2/site; certification practice≥1 non-study subject. Main matching keys: acuity observation, scale, eye-level record and low-vision conversion, not a new analysis model.

### Operational numeric units

Pages110–111 and122–123: light box24¾×25¾×7inches(62.9×65.4×17.8cm);5-pronged wheelbase/prongs14inches(35.6cm),2wheels lockable. Third-row anchor0.8logMAR=45letters=20/125Snellen positioned49±2inches(124.5±5.1cm) above floor. Ambient chart light≤15foot-candles; two20W tubes; output decline5% first100h and another5% next2000h;4days≈96h preburn,replace annually. Sleeve14inches(35.6cm),uncovered ends4 3/16inches(10.6cm). Lanes4m(13feet1.5inches/157.5inches) and1m(39⅜inches); allow7inches(17.8cm) wall-mounted box depth or13inches(33cm) stand caster space. At1m measure to second letter left eye/fourth letter right eye of third row and confirm with1m stick. These are operational calibration details, not trial denominators or statistical hypotheses.

### Cataract and controlled-inflammation scales

Pages125–126: AREDS nuclear/cortical/posterior-subcapsular opacity ratings1clinical presence,2clinical significance,3severe; half grades allowed. Printed categories<1,1,1.5,2,2.5,>3. No-cataract status recorded. This describes prospective grading, not event counts.

Page126 SUN anterior-chamber cells:0<1cell;0.5+1–5;1+6–15;2+16–25;3+26–50;4+>50. Field1mm×1mm; highest magnification/illumination,dark room,before dilation. This scale supports eligibility and controlled-inflammation endpoint interpretation.

Page127 flare:0none;1+faint;2+moderate(iris/lens clear);3+marked(details hazy);4+intense(fibrin/plastic aqueous). Flare is excluded from controlled-inflammation criterion. Vitreous cells:0no cells;0.5+0–5;1+6–10;2+11–20;3+21–50;4+>50;field1mm×0.5mm,dark room,after dilation. Vitreous-cell assessment is also excluded from controlled inflammation. Literal zero/0.5+ labels are retained.

Page127 vitreous haze: indirect ophthalmoscope mid-power,20D lens; NEI used for **all primary and secondary** measures; Miami9-point grading separately, exploratory NEI/Miami correlation and clinical-versus-reading-center photographs. Media opacity making haze unreliable is flagged. Page128 Figure1 NEI:0clear;0.5+slight disc-margin blur with normal nerve-fiber striations/reflex not visualized;1+opacities without obscuring retinal details;2+few opacities/mild optic-nerve/vessel detail blur;3+disc/vessels substantially blurred but visible;4+dense opacity obscuring disc. Dark-room grading. Main matching keys: primary inflammation control, secondary vitreous haze and grading instrument.

## Confirmation outcome and boundaries

Existing assigned-page mapping is confirmed. No changed or new scientifically relevant numeric or inferential relationship was discovered. Chart version labels, literal tumbling-E scoring thresholds and the different letter/tumbling-E testing thresholds were already explicitly present in the current source-linked map; they remain unnormalized. Six OCR axis tables contain dropped or malformed digits/degree symbols, resolved by visually reading all five rows from each retained source rendering. These are derivative transcription limitations with no remaining source-reading gap.

No trial result estimate, confidence interval, P value, fitted statistical model, power formula, sample-size derivation, multiplicity rule or stopping boundary occurs in this assigned scope. No spreadsheet, formula cell, cached workbook value, CSV or Office source is assigned. Administrative/procedural content was opened on every page; applicable observation definitions are mapped above rather than treating such pages as empty. This artifact supplies confirmation evidence only and makes no candidate diagnosis.
